2	
3	**Goal:** Turn user intent into a hardened, reviewable artifact.
4	
5	**CRITICAL:** If a step directs you to another snapshot file, read it fully and follow it. No exceptions.
6

8	If you need an explicit user instruction to run them, ask once now for the whole workflow run.
9	
10	## READY FOR DEVELOPMENT STANDARD
11	
12	A specification is "Ready for Development" when:
13	
14	- **Actionable**: Every task has a file path and specific action.
15	- **Logical**: Tasks ordered by dependency.
16	- **Testable**: All ACs use Given/When/Then.
17	- **Complete**: No placeholders or TBDs.
18	- **Sufficient**: No known requirement, acceptance, dependency, or implementation gaps remain unresolved.
19	- **Coherent**: No unresolved ambiguities or internal contradictions.
20	
21	## SCOPE STANDARD
22	
23	A specification should target a **single user-facing goal** within **900–1600 tokens**:
24	
25	- **Single goal**: One cohesive feature, even if it spans multiple layers/files. Multi-goal means >=2 **top-level independent shippable deliverables** — each could be reviewed, tested, and merged as a separate PR without breaking the others. Never count surface verbs, "and" conjunctions, or noun phrases. Never split cross-layer implementation details inside one user goal.
26	  - Split: "add dark mode toggle AND refactor auth to JWT AND build admin dashboard"
27	  - Don't split: "add validation and display errors" / "support drag-and-drop AND paste AND retry"
28	- **900–1600 tokens**: Optimal range for LLM consumption. Below 900 risks ambiguity; above 1600 risks context-rot in implementation agents.
29	- **Neither limit is a gate.** Both are proposals with user override.
30	
31	## Conventions

33	- Every operational cross-file reference in this workflow is an absolute snapshot path. Open it directly; do not resolve it relative to a skill directory.
34	- `{project-root}`-prefixed paths resolve from the project working directory.

1	# Epic 1 Context: Find a Park
2	
3	<!-- Compiled from planning artifacts. Edit freely. Regenerate with compile-epic-context if planning docs change. -->
4	
5	## Goal
6	
7	Deliver a small, keyless web app (a 2-hour take-home) that lets a resident find a local park in the NYC-area sample set on a map or in a list and open its details, on phone or desktop, by mouse, touch, or keyboard, with missing data handled honestly. The interviewer must be able to run it with one install and one start command and judge it from the README. A modest app that works and is well explained beats an ambitious one with broken core features; stop at 2 hours and document what is unfinished.
8	
9	## Stories
10	
11	- Story 1.1: Scaffold app and park data layer
12	- Story 1.2: Park list with keyboard selection
13	- Story 1.3: Park details with missing-data handling
14	- Story 1.4: Map of parks synced with selection
15	- Story 1.5: Responsive and accessibility polish with design applied
16	- Story 1.6: Tests and submission README
17	
18	## Requirements & Constraints
19	
20	- **Cross-cutting (every story):** no geolocation ever requested; no credentials or API keys; never invent missing data; `parks.sample.json` is never edited (copied into the app byte-for-byte); keyboard focus always visible.
21	- The list plus details view must be a complete alternative to the map. All 12 parks appear in the list, including ones with missing fields; parks without valid lat/lng get no marker but stay in the list and details.
22	- Every one of the 12 parks renders; null/missing/empty fields are omitted or labelled, never crash the UI. Failed images degrade without a broken-image icon.
23	- A data-load failure shows an error message with retry, never a blank screen.
24	- At 375px and desktop widths, list → details → back works with no horizontal scroll.
25	- Unredacted AI transcripts (Claude Code, exported by the developer before each fresh story context, and Claude Design) and design files are part of the submission; the README must say where they live.
26	- Non-goals: backend, accounts, deployment, data editing. Search, filters, current location only if core is done with time to spare.
27	- Where the design and the spec disagree, the spec wins.
28	
29	## Technical Decisions
30	
31	- **Stack:** React + Vite, Tailwind CSS (mobile-first), `react-leaflet` + Leaflet with OpenStreetMap standard tiles (keyless, attribution visible), Vitest + React Testing Library. No component library.
32	- **Data loading (readiness decision):** the spec says static import, but loading/error/retry states require a mechanism that can actually fail. Use one that can (e.g. `fetch` from `public/` or a dynamic `import()`) and document the choice in the README.
33	- **Routing (readiness decision):** React Router. `/` is the list, `/parks/:id` is details (supersedes the design's `?park=` option). Unknown id shows "park not found" with a way back. Browser Back must work.
34	- **Data shape:** array of park objects with `id`, `name` (treat as required); `description`, `location.{lat,lng,address}`, `amenities` (kebab-case tokens), `hours` (free text, shown as written), `images` (URL array), `acreage`, `rating` (0–5) may each be missing, null, or empty. Read nested fields null-safely (`park.location?.address`).
35	- **Normalization:** one null-safe module applies safe defaults with no fabricated values, sorts parks A–Z and numbers them 1–N; that number is shared by list cards and map markers. Centralize fallback rules in a small helper (e.g. `display(value, fallback)`).
36	- **Image URL transformation:** pixel art moves from `design/park-images/` to `src/assets/park-images/`. At load time each image URL maps by filename stem to the bundled asset (`.../prospect-1.jpg` → `park-images/prospect-1.png`); unmatched URLs pass through unchanged so `onError` fallback still applies. Do not mutate source data. Document in README.
37	- **Amenity labels:** replace `-` with a space and capitalize the first letter (`do
... [4122 more chars]

13	<frozen-after-approval reason="human-owned intent — do not modify unless human renegotiates">
14	
15	## Intent
16	
17	**Problem:** The `/` route shows only an "All parks" heading. A resident cannot browse parks or open one without a map, and the list is the main way to choose a park (CAP-2).
18	
19	**Approach:** Replace the list placeholder with an "All parks" heading, a one-line hint, and a `<ul>` of 12 park cards, sorted A–Z by the existing loader. Each card is a single React Router `<Link>` to `/parks/:id`. When the user comes back to `/` from a park, focus returns to that park's card.
20	
21	## Boundaries & Constraints
22	
23	**Always:** use the normalized parks from `useParks` as they are (order and `number` come from the loader, never recomputed); never invent data; one Tab stop per card; amber `focus-ring` on every card; touch target ≥ 44px; Tailwind tokens from `src/index.css`.
24	
25	**Never:** details content, the "All parks" back control or focus-to-heading on details (Story 1.3); map or markers (1.4); desktop sidebar/phone toggle layout (1.5); clickable `<div>`s; a nested interactive element inside a card.
26	
27	## I/O & Edge-Case Matrix
28	
29	| Scenario | Input / State | Expected Output / Behavior | Error Handling |
30	|----------|--------------|---------------------------|----------------|
31	| Happy path | 12 sample parks | 12 `<li>` cards in loader order, badges 1–12 | N/A |
32	| Missing address | Highland Dog Park `address: null` | "Address not listed" in `text-muted` | N/A |
33	| > 3 amenities | Prospect Park (7) | first 3 tags + "+4 more" | N/A |
34	| ≤ 3 amenities | East Ridge (3) | 3 tags, no "+N more" | N/A |
35	| No amenities | `amenities: []` | no tag row | N/A |
36	| Amenity label | `dog-run`, `wifi` | "Dog run", "Wifi" | unknown tokens humanized, never dropped |
37	| Return from details | `/parks/highland-dog-park` → Back | Highland card has focus | N/A |
38	| First visit | open `/` directly | no card is auto-focused | N/A |
39	
40	</frozen-after-approval>
41	
42	## Code Map
43	
44	- `src/App.jsx` -- `ParkListPlaceholder` (renders only the h1) is what this story replaces; `ParksPanel` owns the `<Routes>` and receives `parks`; `ParkDetailsPlaceholder` renders on `/parks/:id`. Keep the loading/error states, `NotFound`, `cardClass`, and the landmarks.
45	- `src/data/parks.js` -- normalized park: `{ id, name, number, address, amenities, ... }`; `amenities` is always an array of strings. Don't change normalization.
46	- `src/index.css` -- tokens (`tag-bg`, `tag-fg`, `muted`, `forest`, `line`, `surface`) and the `focus-ring` utility.
47	- `src/App.test.jsx` -- `renderAt(path)` + `okResponse()` + the `TestNav` pattern for history Back; extend it here.
48	- `design/screens/01-desktop-list-map.png`, `04-phone-list.png` -- card visual: forest round number badge, 17px bold name, muted address, rounded tags. Hint copy: "Choose a park to see hours, amenities and directions."
49	
50	## Tasks & Acceptance
51

18	**Problem:** There is no app yet. Every later story needs a runnable Vite + React + Tailwind project, park data that cannot crash the UI, routes, a layout shell, and bundled park images.
19

21	
22	## Boundaries & Constraints
23	
24	**Always:** one `npm install` + `npm run dev`; no API keys; `requirements/parks.sample.json` copied byte-for-byte and never edited; normalization never fabricates values and never mutates the parsed source; nested reads null-safe; tokens, the Public Sans font and the amber focus ring come from `design/README.md`.

import { useCallback, useEffect, useState } from 'react'
import { Link, Route, Routes, useParams } from 'react-router'
import ParkList from './components/ParkList.jsx'
import { useParks } from './data/useParks.js'

const cardClass = 'rounded-[10px] border border-line bg-surface p-4'
const linkClass = 'focus-ring rounded text-forest underline'

function NotFound() {
  return (
    <div>
      <h1 className="text-lg font-bold">Park not found</h1>
      <p className="mt-2">
        <Link to="/" className={linkClass}>
          Back to all parks
        </Link>
      </p>
    </div>
  )
}

function ParkDetailsPlaceholder({ parks, onOpen }) {
  const { id } = useParams()
  const park = parks.find((p) => p.id === id)

  // Record the opened park so the list can return focus to its card.
  useEffect(() => {
    if (park) onOpen(park.id)
  }, [park, onOpen])

  if (!park) return <NotFound />

  return <h1 className="text-[28px] font-extrabold">{park.name}</h1>
}

function ParksPanel({ status, parks, retry, lastParkId, setLastParkId }) {
  const clearLastParkId = useCallback(() => setLastParkId(null), [setLastParkId])

  if (status === 'loading') {
    return (
      <p role="status" className={cardClass}>
        Loading parks…
      </p>
    )
  }

  if (status === 'error') {
    return (
      <div role="alert" className={cardClass}>
        <p>Couldn't load parks. Try again.</p>
        <button
          type="button"
          onClick={retry}
          className="focus-ring mt-3 min-h-11 rounded-lg bg-forest px-4 font-semibold text-white hover:bg-forest-dark"
        >
          Retry
        </button>
      </div>
    )
  }

  return (
    <Routes>
      <Route
        path="/"
        element={
          <ParkList parks={parks} focusParkId={lastParkId} onFocusUsed={clearLastParkId} />
        }
      />
      <Route
        path="/parks/:id"
        element={<ParkDetailsPlaceholder parks={parks} onOpen={setLastParkId} />}
      />
      <Route path="*" element={<NotFound />} />
    </Routes>
  )
}

export default function App() {
  const { status, parks, retry } = useParks()
  // Id of the last-opened park. Lives in App state (not location.state) so
  // focus can return to its card after browser Back, which carries no state.
  const [lastParkId, setLastParkId] = useState(null)

  return (
    <div className="flex min-h-screen flex-col">
      <header className="bg-forest px-5 py-4 text-white">
        <p className="text-xl font-e
... [1468 more chars]

# Find a Park: design handoff

UI designs for the Find a Park take-home. They were drawn on a Claude design canvas before any code was written, and they are the visual spec for the React + Tailwind + Leaflet build. The full AI transcript of the design session is in the repo alongside this folder.

**These are reference designs, not code.** Build with React, Tailwind and react-leaflet (no component library), and use the screenshots plus the rules below. Where the brief and these designs disagree, the brief wins.

## Contents

| File | What it shows |
|---|---|
| `screens/01-desktop-list-map.png` | Desktop default: park list on the left, map on the right |
| `screens/02-desktop-park-selected.png` | Desktop with a park selected: details replace the list, the marker is highlighted |
| `screens/03-desktop-missing-data.png` | Desktop details for a park with missing data (Cedar Hill: no photos, no rating) |
| `screens/04-phone-list.png` | Phone, List tab |
| `screens/05-phone-map-selected.png` | Phone, Map tab, with a park selected (preview card) |
| `screens/06-phone-details.png` | Phone details, full data (Prospect Park) |
| `screens/07-phone-missing-data.png` | Phone details, missing data (Cedar Hill) |
| `source/*.dc.html` | Canvas source files, for reference only. They need the canvas runtime and are **not** meant to run in the app. |

Screenshots are at 2x: desktop at 1280×800, phone at 390×844. The map in every screenshot is a drawn stand-in. The real app uses Leaflet with OpenStreetMap tiles.

## Layout and behaviour

### Desktop (≥ 768px, Tailwind `md:`)

- A green header with the app name, a subtitle and the park count.
- Two columns:
  - **Left sidebar (~400px):** scrolls on its own.
  - **Map:** fills the rest of the screen.
- **Sidebar, list mode:** an "All parks" heading, a one-line hint, then one card per park.
- **Selecting a park** from either the list or the map:
  - The sidebar switches to details mode, starting with an "All parks" back button.
  - That park's marker turns amber and gets bigger.
  - The URL should change too (e.g. `?park=prospect-park` or `/parks/:id`), so details can be linked and the browser Back button works.
- **Going back to the list:** move focus to the card of the park you came from.
- **Opening details:** move focus to the details heading, so keyboard and screen-reader users land in the right place.

### Phone (< 768px)

- One column. A **List / Map** toggle sits under the header, made of two real `<button>`s with `aria-pressed`. List is the default.
- **Tapping a marker** opens a preview card at the bottom of the map. It shows the park's position in the list ("Park 8 of 12"), its name, address and hours, a **View park details** button and a close button.
- **Details** take the whole screen, with an "All parks" back link in the header.

### List ↔ map link

- Parks are sorted A–Z and numbered 1–12. The same number appears on the list card and on the map marker, so the two views always match.
- Every marker is focusable and named "8. Prospect Park" for screen readers. Use `L.divIcon` with a `<button>` inside it, or set `keyboard: true` and `alt`/`title` on the markers.
- The list is the main way to choose a park. The map is never the only way.

## Park card (list)

- Number badge, park name (17px bold), address, and the first three amenities as tags. If there are more, add a "+N more" tag.
- The whole card is a single `<button>` (or a link once there is routing), so it gets keyboard focus and one Tab stop.

## Details

The details view shows, from top to bottom:

1. Photo area (see the missing-data rules below)
2. Park name as the page heading (26–28px, 800 weight), then the address
3. An **Open in OpenStreetMap** link built from the park's lat/lng (`https://www.openstreetmap.org/?mlat={lat}&mlon={lng}#map=16/{lat}/{lng}`). This was added during design and is easy to drop if you don't want it.
4. Three fact tiles: **Hours**, **Size** (acres), **Rating** ("4.7 / 5")
5. **About** (the descript
... [12892 more chars]

15	## Intent
16	
17	**Problem:** The "Map of parks" region is a "Map coming soon." placeholder. Residents cannot see where the parks are or pick one by location.
18	
19	**Approach:** Render a react-leaflet map with OpenStreetMap tiles, fitted to the parks that have coordinates. Each such park gets a numbered, focusable marker that uses the loader's `number`. The park on `/parks/:id` has an amber, larger marker. At ≥ 768px, activating a marker navigates to `/parks/:id`. At < 768px it opens a preview card instead.
20	
21	## Boundaries & Constraints
22	
23	**Always:** use loader `number`/order unchanged; `hasCoords` decides which parks get markers; visible OSM attribution; one Tab stop per marker, a real `<button>`, name "N. Park name", `focus-ring`; touch targets ≥ 44px; preview copy shows a missing address as "Address not listed" and missing hours as "Hours not listed"; no API keys.
24	
25	**Never:** geolocation (`navigator.geolocation`, `locate()`); a desktop two-column sidebar, a phone List/Map toggle, or any layout restyling (Story 1.5); details content (Story 1.3); panning the map on selection; editing `parks.sample.json`.
26

28	
29	| Scenario | Input / State | Expected Output / Behavior | Error Handling |

33	| No coords | a park with `hasCoords: false` | no marker; still in the list | N/A |
34	| Desktop activate | ≥ 768px, click or Enter on a marker | URL `/parks/:id`, park heading in aside | N/A |
35	| Phone tap | < 768px, tap marker 8 | preview: "Park 8 of 12", name, address, hours, "View park details", "Close park preview"; marker 8 amber; URL unchanged | N/A |
36	| Preview missing address | < 768px, Highland Dog Park | "Address not listed" (muted) | N/A |
37	| Preview → details | press "View park details" | navigates to `/parks/:id` | N/A |
38	| Preview close | press "Close park preview" | preview gone; focus back on that marker | N/A |
39	| Not loaded | loading or error | map region shows no map | N/A |

# Epic 1 Context: Find a Park

<!-- Compiled from planning artifacts. Edit freely. Regenerate with compile-epic-context if planning docs change. -->

## Goal

Deliver a small, keyless web app (a 2-hour take-home) that lets a resident find a local park in the NYC-area sample set on a map or in a list and open its details, on phone or desktop, by mouse, touch, or keyboard, with missing data handled honestly. The interviewer must be able to run it with one install and one start command and judge it from the README. A modest app that works and is well explained beats an ambitious one with broken core features; stop at 2 hours and document what is unfinished.

## Stories

- Story 1.1: Scaffold app and park data layer
- Story 1.2: Park list with keyboard selection
- Story 1.3: Park details with missing-data handling
- Story 1.4: Map of parks synced with selection
- Story 1.5: Responsive and accessibility polish with design applied
- Story 1.6: Tests and submission README

## Requirements & Constraints

- **Cross-cutting (every story):** no geolocation ever requested; no credentials or API keys; never invent missing data; `parks.sample.json` is never edited (copied into the app byte-for-byte); keyboard focus always visible.
- The list plus details view must be a complete alternative to the map. All 12 parks appear in the list, including ones with missing fields; parks without valid lat/lng get no marker but stay in the list and details.
- Every one of the 12 parks renders; null/missing/empty fields are omitted or labelled, never crash the UI. Failed images degrade without a broken-image icon.
- A data-load failure shows an error message with retry, never a blank screen.
- At 375px and desktop widths, list → details → back works with no horizontal scroll.
- Unredacted AI transcripts (Claude Code, exported by the developer before each fresh story context, and Claude Design) and design files are part of the submission; the README must say where they live.
- Non-goals: backend, accounts, deployment, data editing. Search, filters, current location only if core is done with time to spare.
- Where the design and the spec disagree, the spec wins.

## Technical Decisions

- **Stack:** React + Vite, Tailwind CSS (mobile-first), `react-leaflet` + Leaflet with OpenStreetMap standard tiles (keyless, attribution visible), Vitest + React Testing Library. No component library.
- **Data loading (readiness decision):** the spec says static import, but loading/error/retry states require a mechanism that can actually fail. Use one that can (e.g. `fetch` from `public/` or a dynamic `import()`) and document the choice in the README.
- **Routing (readiness decision):** React Router. `/` is the list, `/parks/:id` is details (supersedes the design's `?park=` option). Unknown id shows "park not found" with a way back. Browser Back must work.
- **Data shape:** array of park objects with `id`, `name` (treat as required); `description`, `location.{lat,lng,address}`, `amenities` (kebab-case tokens), `hours` (free text, shown as written), `images` (URL array), `acreage`, `rating` (0–5) may each be missing, null, or empty. Read nested fields null-safely (`park.location?.address`).
- **Normalization:** one null-safe module applies safe defaults with no fabricated values, sorts parks A–Z and numbers them 1–N; that number is shared by list cards and map markers. Centralize fallback rules in a small helper (e.g. `display(value, fallback)`).
- **Image URL transformation:** pixel art moves from `design/park-images/` to `src/assets/park-images/`. At load time each image URL maps by filename stem to the bundled asset (`.../prospect-1.jpg` → `park-images/prospect-1.png`); unmatched URLs pass through unchanged so `onError` fallback still applies. Do not mutate source data. Document in README.
- **Amenity labels:** replace `-` with a space and capitalize the first letter (`dog-run` → "Dog run"); unknown tokens are humanized, never dropped.
... [12394 more chars]

import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs'
import { resolve } from 'node:path'
import { fireEvent, render, screen, within } from '@testing-library/react'
import userEvent from '@testing-library/user-event'
import { MemoryRouter, useNavigate } from 'react-router'
import { afterEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest'
import App from './App.jsx'

const sample = JSON.parse(
  readFileSync(resolve(process.cwd(), 'public/data/parks.sample.json'), 'utf8'),
)

const okResponse = () => ({ ok: true, json: async () => structuredClone(sample) })
const ERROR_TEXT = "Couldn't load parks. Try again."

function renderAt(path) {
  return render(
    <MemoryRouter initialEntries={[path]}>
      <App />
    </MemoryRouter>,
  )
}

afterEach(() => vi.unstubAllGlobals())

describe('App shell', () => {
  it('goes loading → error → retry → ready', async () => {
    let rejectFirst
    const fetchMock = vi
      .fn()
      .mockImplementationOnce(
        () =>
          new Promise((_resolve, reject) => {
            rejectFirst = reject
          }),
      )
      .mockResolvedValueOnce(okResponse())
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', fetchMock)
    const user = userEvent.setup()

    renderAt('/')
    const aside = screen.getByRole('complementary', { name: 'Parks' })
    expect(within(aside).getByText('Loading parks…')).toBeInTheDocument()

    rejectFirst(new TypeError('network down'))

import { Fragment, useEffect, useRef } from 'react'
import { Link } from 'react-router'
import { amenityLabel } from '../data/format.js'

const MAX_TAGS = 3

function ParkCard({ park, linkRef }) {
  const shown = park.amenities.slice(0, MAX_TAGS)
  const extra = park.amenities.length - shown.length
  const tags = shown.map(amenityLabel)
  if (extra > 0) tags.push(`+${extra} more`)

  return (
    <li>
      <Link
        ref={linkRef}
        to={`/parks/${park.id}`}
        className="focus-ring flex min-h-11 items-start gap-3 rounded-[10px] border border-line bg-surface p-4 hover:border-forest"
      >
        <span
          aria-hidden="true"
          className="flex size-8 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-forest text-sm font-bold text-white"
        >
          {park.number}
        </span>
        <span className="sr-only">{park.number}.</span>{' '}
        <span className="min-w-0 flex-1">
          <span className="block text-[17px] font-bold">{park.name}</span>{' '}
          {park.address ? (
            <span className="block text-muted">{park.address}</span>
          ) : (
            <span className="block text-muted">Address not listed</span>
          )}{' '}
          {tags.length > 0 && (
            <span data-testid="park-tags" className="mt-2 flex flex-wrap gap-2">
              {tags.map((label, index) => (
                <Fragment key={`${index}-${label}`}>
                  <span className="rounded-full bg-tag-bg px-2.5 py-0.5 text-sm text-tag-fg">
                    {label}
                  </span>{' '}
                </Fragment>
              ))}
            </span>
          )}
        </span>
        <svg
          aria-hidden="true"
          viewBox="0 0 24 24"
          className="size-5 shrink-0 self-center text-muted"
          fill="none"
          stroke="currentColor"
          strokeWidth="2"
        >
          <path d="m9 6 6 6-6 6" />
        </svg>
      </Link>
    </li>
  )
}

    - The selected id comes from `useMatch('/parks/:id')`.
    - `previewId` is only used below 768px. It is cleared on any navigation and when the viewport becomes desktop.

  **Risks and things to know**
  - **Existing tests edited:** four Story 1.3 details tests used `document.querySelector('img')`. The map's tile images now match that, so I scoped those queries to the details `<article>`.
  - **Layout:** the map section lost `flex-1` and the map is a fixed 480px tall. That was the smallest explicit height Leaflet needs; Story 1.5 restyles the layout.
  - **`isDesktop` unused:** `ParkMap` accepts `isDesktop` as the spec's signature says, but the map renders the same at every width, so it doesn't use it.
  - **Extra Tab stops:** Leaflet's own controls stay focusable — the map container, the zoom buttons and the attribution links.
  - **Mouse focus:** a mouse press inside the map moves focus to the map container first. Keyboard activation is unaffected, and after a click focus ends on the details heading (desktop) or the preview card (phone).
  - **Phone while viewing a park:** on `/parks/:id`, the selected park keeps the amber highlight even when a different marker's preview is open, because the spec's `selectedId ?? previewId` rule puts the route first.
  - **Preview "Hours" wording:** the design shows "Open 6:00 AM - 1:00 AM". I show the hours as written, with a screen-reader-only "Hours:" prefix instead.
  - **Test output:** jsdom prints harmless "Window's scrollTo() not implemented" warnings when the map takes focus in tests.

+- Given a production build, when markers render, then no broken default marker icon appears (divIcon only).
+
+## Implementation Notes
+
+- Marker focus return after "Close park preview" is driven by a `focusId`/`onFocused` pair on `ParkMap`: closing changes the highlight, react-leaflet then swaps the marker's divIcon (replacing its DOM), so the map focuses the new button in an effect that runs after the markers' own effects. Icons are memoised per park and state so unrelated renders never replace marker DOM.
+- `ParkMap` accepts `isDesktop` per the contract but renders identically at every width; App decides what activation does.
+- The preview is a `<section tabIndex=-1 aria-labelledby>` overlaid at the bottom of the map (`z-[1001]`, above Leaflet controls; `bottom-8` keeps the attribution visible). Escape also closes it.

1	# Verification Gap Review
2	
3	**Goal:** Find changed behavior that could break without reliable verification catching it. Ask one question — "if the behavior this change is supposed to produce broke where it's actually used, would verification fail?" Do not hunt for correctness bugs, but report genuine problems you notice while tracing verification.
4	
5	The main verification gap shapes are:
6	
7	1. **Regression gap:** the changed code regresses where it's used, and no test covering that use would fail.
8	2. **Missing-adoption gap:** a place that should now use the new behavior doesn't; it handles the same case its own way, or not at all, and no test would flag the omission.
9	3. **Broken-verification gap:** a test appears to cover the changed behavior, but would not actually protect it because it is skipped, flaky, not run in the normal verification path, or too weak to observe the regression.
10	
11	## Evidence Rules
12	
13	- Read a test before claiming what it covers, runs, asserts, or misses.
14	- Before claiming no test exists, search the whole repo by the symbol under test and by import references; expected file locations are not enough.
15	- Never assert what you did not verify. If a finding cannot be grounded, drop it.
16	- In a finding, say what you actually checked — "none of the tests I read cover this" — and show how far you looked. Say a test doesn't exist anywhere only when the symbol/import-reference search actually shows that.
17	- Do not assign severity, confidence, priority, or ranking.
18	
19	## Review Sequence
20	
21	### Step 1: Screen for behavioral change
22	
23	Screen each part of the change separately. If a part is non-behavioral, skip it. Call a part non-behavioral only when the changed code does not alter return values, thrown errors, caller-visible side effects, or observable state (including iteration order and emitted messages). Once a part meets that test, move on; do not inspect callers or tests for extra confirmation.
24	
25	Common non-behavioral examples: formatting, comments, whitespace; pure renames; trivial getters/setters and pass-throughs; type-only or compiler-enforced changes with no runtime effect; etc.
26	
27	Only outcomes produced by deterministic code are worth automatically testing; tests are useless on static source text and brittle on LLM output. Skip those parts.
28	
29	If every part is skipped, output the clean result (see Output Format).
30	
31	### Step 2: Find the behavior that changed
32	
33	Identify what behavior changed compared to the previous version: output, side effect, branch, error path, schema/event shape, config default, validation/authorization rule, external contract, etc. If the change affects more than one behavior, handle each separately.
34	
35	Treat broad-impact changes as behavioral even when no single changed line looks important: dependency, toolchain, build/config, data-file, etc.
36	
37	### Step 3: Trace where that behavior is used
38	
39	Trace the changed behavior to the places that observe it. Start with direct callers and registered entry points (routes, commands, DI), contract consumers (schemas, events, APIs, database readers), and reverse-dependency info if already available.
40	
41	Follow a path only while the changed behavior is reachable and unverified. Stop when a test at that boundary would fail, the consumer does not observe the changed behavior, or the next hop is guesswork (dynamic dispatch, reflection, outside-repo consumers, etc.). Prefer the nearest observable boundary, often one to three hops away, especially across contract, integration, or service edges. If there are more than five similar consumers, group obvious repeats and check representative paths; expand only when a consumer observes the behavior differently.
42	
43	### Step 4: Qualify the consumer, then check its test
44	
45	For each consumer, name the smallest realistic regression this consumer would observe: invert the branch, drop the default, omit the field, return the o
... [6135 more chars]

===== 2026-10-15T06:03:13.937000+00:00  [main]  assistant =====
Story 1-4 is implemented on `story/1-4-map-of-parks-synced-with-selection` and is now in review. All 101 tests pass and the production build succeeds. Nothing is committed yet, and nobody has opened the map in a real browser.

**What's built:**
- An OpenStreetMap map with visible attribution, zoomed to fit the parks that have coordinates. It never asks for the user's location.
- One marker per park, showing the same number as its list card. Each marker is a single keyboard stop with the amber focus ring and is read as "N. Park name".
- The park currently open at `/parks/:id` gets a larger amber marker.
- At 768px and wider, clicking a marker or pressing Enter on it opens that park's details.
- Below 768px, tapping a marker opens a preview card instead of navigating. It shows "Park N of 12", the name, address and hours, a "View park details" link and a "Close park preview" button. Closing it puts focus back on the marker, and Escape also closes it.
- Every row of the spec's edge-case table has a test that passes.

**Things you might notice:**
- **Map placement:** until story 1.5, the map sits under the list at every width at a fixed 480px tall.
- **Story 1-3 tests changed:** four 1-3 details tests had to be narrowed to the details panel, because the map's tile images also match their image query.
- **Extra Tab stops:** Leaflet's zoom buttons and attribution links are also in the Tab order.
- **Highlight conflict on phone:** while viewing a park, opening another marker's preview leaves the open park's marker amber, not the previewed one.

Three independent reviewers are checking the diff now. When they're done, I'll sort their findings, fix the real ones and report back.

/** Human label for a kebab-case amenity token: `dog-run` → "Dog run". Unknown tokens are humanized, never dropped. */
export function amenityLabel(token) {
  const spaced = String(token ?? '').trim().replace(/-/g, ' ')
  return spaced.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + spaced.slice(1)
}

/** Rating in 0–5 as "4.7 / 5" (one decimal); `null` when there is no valid rating. */
export function formatRating(n) {
  if (typeof n !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(n)) return null
  return `${n.toFixed(1)} / 5`
}

/** Acreage as "526 acres" or "1 acre"; `null` when there is no valid acreage. */
export function formatAcres(n) {
  if (typeof n !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(n)) return null
  return `${n} ${n === 1 ? 'acre' : 'acres'}`
}

/** OpenStreetMap link centred on a point. */
export function osmUrl(lat, lng) {
  return `https://www.openstreetmap.org/?mlat=${lat}&mlon=${lng}#map=16/${lat}/${lng}`
}

/**
 * The one fallback rule: a value that is null, undefined, blank text or a non-finite number is
 * missing, and shows the labelled fallback instead. Never invents a value.
 */
export function display(value, fallback) {
  const missing =
    value === null ||
    value === undefined ||
    (typeof value === 'string' && value.trim() === '') ||
    (typeof value === 'number' && !Number.isFinite(value))
  return missing ? { text: fallback, missing: true } : { text: String(value), missing: false }
}
// Null-safe park data layer. Normalization never invents values and never
// mutates the parsed source: missing scalars become `null`, missing arrays `[]`.

const imageModules = import.meta.glob('../assets/park-images/*.png', {
  eager: true,
  query: '?url',
  import: 'default',
})

// filename stem (e.g. "prospect-1") -> bundled asset URL
const bundledImages = Object.fromEntries(
  Object.entries(imageModules).map(([path, url]) => [fileStem(path), url]),
)

function fileStem(pathOrUrl) {
  const withoutQuery = pathOrUrl.split(/[?#]/)[0]
  const file = withoutQuery.split('/').pop() ?? ''
  const dot = file.lastIndexOf('.')
  return dot > 0 ? file.slice(0, dot) : file
}

/** Map a source image URL to the bundled art with the same filename stem; unmatched URLs pass through unchanged. */
export function resolveImageUrl(url) {
  if (typeof url !== 'string') return url
  return bundledImages[fileStem(url)] ?? url
}

function text(value) {
  if (typeof value !== 'string') return null
  const trimmed = value.trim()
  return trimmed !== '' ? trimmed : null
}

function finiteNumber(value) {
  return typeof value === 'number' && Number.isFinite(value) ? value : null
}

function numberInRange(value, min, max) {
  const n = finiteNumber(value)
  return n !== null && n >= min && n <= max ? n : null
}

function stringList(value) {
  return Array.isArray(value) ? value.filter((item) => text(item) !== null) : []
}

export function normalizePark(raw) {
  const park = raw && typeof raw === 'object' ? raw : {}
  const location = park.location && typeof park.location === 'object' ? park.location : {}
  const lat = numberInRange(location.lat, -90, 90)
  const lng = numberInRange(location.lng, -180, 180)
  const acreage = finiteNumber(park.acreage)
  const rating = finiteNumber(park.rating)

  return {
    id: text(park.id),
    name: text(park.name),
    number: null,
    description: text(park.description),
    address: text(location.address),
    lat,
    lng,
    hasCoords: lat !== null && lng !== null,
    amenities: stringList(park.amenities),
    hours: text(park.hours),
    images: stringList(park.images).map(resolveImageUrl),
    acreage: acreage !== null && acreage >= 0 ? acreage : null,
    rating: rating !== null && rating >= 0 && rating <= 5 ? rating : null,
  }
}

function byName(a, b) {
  if (a.name === null) return b.name === null ? 0 : 1
  if (b.name === null) return -1
  return a.name.localeCompare(b.name, 'en', { sensitivity: 'base' })
}

<frozen-after-approval reason="human-owned intent — do not modify unless human renegotiates">

## Intent

**Problem:** The "Map of parks" region is a "Map coming soon." placeholder. Residents cannot see where the parks are or pick one by location.

**Approach:** Render a react-leaflet map with OpenStreetMap tiles, fitted to the parks that have coordinates. Each such park gets a numbered, focusable marker that uses the loader's `number`. The park on `/parks/:id` has an amber, larger marker. At ≥ 768px, activating a marker navigates to `/parks/:id`. At < 768px it opens a preview card instead.

## Boundaries & Constraints

**Always:** use loader `number`/order unchanged; `hasCoords` decides which parks get markers; visible OSM attribution; one Tab stop per marker, a real `<button>`, name "N. Park name", `focus-ring`; touch targets ≥ 44px; preview copy shows a missing address as "Address not listed" and missing hours as "Hours not listed"; no API keys.

**Never:** geolocation (`navigator.geolocation`, `locate()`); a desktop two-column sidebar, a phone List/Map toggle, or any layout restyling (Story 1.5); details content (Story 1.3); panning the map on selection; editing `parks.sample.json`.

## I/O & Edge-Case Matrix

| Scenario | Input / State | Expected Output / Behavior | Error Handling |
|----------|--------------|---------------------------|----------------|
| Happy path | 12 sample parks at `/` | 12 marker buttons named "1. …" to "12. …", none amber | N/A |
| Selected | `/parks/prospect-park` | Prospect Park's marker amber and larger; all others forest | N/A |
| No coords | a park with `hasCoords: false` | no marker; still in the list | N/A |
| Desktop activate | ≥ 768px, click or Enter on a marker | URL `/parks/:id`, park heading in aside | N/A |
| Phone tap | < 768px, tap marker 8 | preview: "Park 8 of 12", name, address, hours, "View park details", "Close park preview"; marker 8 amber; URL unchanged | N/A |
| Preview missing address | < 768px, Highland Dog Park | "Address not listed" (muted) | N/A |
| Preview → details | press "View park details" | navigates to `/parks/:id` | N/A |
| Preview close | press "Close park preview" | preview gone; focus back on that marker | N/A |
| Not loaded | loading or error | map region shows no map | N/A |

</frozen-after-approval>

import { useCallback, useEffect, useState } from 'react'
import { loadParks } from './parks.js'

/** Loads parks once; `retry` refetches after an error. */
export function useParks() {
  const [attempt, setAttempt] = useState(0)
  const [state, setState] = useState({ status: 'loading', parks: [] })

  useEffect(() => {
    let cancelled = false
    setState({ status: 'loading', parks: [] })
    loadParks().then(
      (parks) => {
        if (!cancelled) setState({ status: 'ready', parks })
      },
      () => {
        if (!cancelled) setState({ status: 'error', parks: [] })
      },
    )
    return () => {
      cancelled = true
    }
  }, [attempt])

  const retry = useCallback(() => setAttempt((n) => n + 1), [])

  return { status: state.status, parks: state.parks, retry }
}
17:        to={`/parks/${park.id}`}

## Intent

**Problem:** The "Map of parks" region is a "Map coming soon." placeholder. Residents cannot see where the parks are or pick one by location.

**Approach:** Render a react-leaflet map with OpenStreetMap tiles, fitted to the parks that have coordinates. Each such park gets a numbered, focusable marker that uses the loader's `number`. The park on `/parks/:id` has an amber, larger marker. At ≥ 768px, activating a marker navigates to `/parks/:id`. At < 768px it opens a preview card instead.

===== 2026-10-15T06:06:28.619000+00:00  [agent-acbee6ceabb68807d (general-purpose)]  assistant =====
I fixed all four review items. I only ran the two test files that cover the edited code (`src/App.test.jsx` and `src/components/ParkMap.test.jsx`), and both pass (65 tests).

1. **Escape closes the preview (test only):** added a phone test in `A:\Source\find-local-parks\src\App.test.jsx`, "closes the preview on Escape and returns focus to the marker". It opens marker 8's preview, checks the preview has focus, presses `{Escape}`, then checks the preview is gone and focus is on the "8. Prospect Park" marker.

2. **Focus when the viewport switches to desktop:** in `A:\Source\find-local-parks\src\App.jsx`, the `isDesktop` effect now runs only when a preview is open. It sets `focusMarkerId` to that preview's id before clearing it, the same way `closePreview` does. The effect now also depends on `previewId`. The test "clears the preview when the viewport becomes desktop" now also checks the preview has focus before the switch and the Prospect Park marker has it after.

3. **Tile URL:** in `A:\Source\find-local-parks\src\components\ParkMap.jsx`, `TILE_URL` is now `https://tile.openstreetmap.org/{z}/{x}/{y}.png`. In `A:\Source\find-local-parks\src\components\ParkMap.test.jsx`, the tile test now requires `^https://tile\.openstreetmap\.org/\d+/\d+/\d+\.png$`, so a subdomain fails it.

4. **No duplicate history entry on desktop:** in `A:\Source\find-local-parks\src\App.jsx`, `activateMarker` skips `navigate` when `id === selectedId`, and `selectedId` was added to its dependencies. A new desktop test, "does not push a duplicate history entry for the already-selected marker", opens `/` and clicks Prospect Park's marker, then clicks it again. A single Back (`navigate(-1)`) then returns to `/` with the "All parks" heading showing.

I checked that each new or extended test catches its bug. I removed Escape handling from the preview's `onKeyDown`, then dropped the `selectedId` guard and the `setFocusMarkerId` call from the `isDesktop` effect. The three matching tests failed, and passed again once I put the code back.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
merge_session_transcripts.py

Collect the main transcript and every subagent transcript for a Claude Code
session ID, then interleave them into one timeline sorted by timestamp.

Claude Code stores sessions under ~/.claude/projects/<project-slug>/:
    <session-id>.jsonl                       main conversation
    <session-id>/subagents/agent-*.jsonl     each subagent's full transcript
    <session-id>/**/*.jsonl                  any other agent transcripts (picked up too)

Usage:
    python3 merge_session_transcripts.py <session-id>
    python3 merge_session_transcripts.py <session-id> -f md -o session.md
    python3 merge_session_transcripts.py <session-id> -f jsonl -o merged.jsonl
    python3 merge_session_transcripts.py <session-id> --projects-dir /path/to/.claude/projects

Formats:
    text   readable plain text (default)
    md     Markdown, one heading per message
    jsonl  the original JSON entries, merged, each tagged with "_source"
"""

import argparse
import json
import sys
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from pathlib import Path

DEFAULT_PROJECTS_DIR = Path.home() / ".claude" / "projects"
MIN_TS = datetime.min.replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- discovery

def find_session_files(session_id: str, projects_dir: Path):
    """Return (main_file or None, [agent files]) for the session ID."""
    mains = sorted(projects_dir.glob(f"*/{session_id}.jsonl"))
    session_dirs = sorted(p for p in projects_dir.glob(f"*/{session_id}") if p.is_dir())

    if not mains and not session_dirs:
        return None, []

    if len(mains) > 1:
        print(f"warning: session found in {len(mains)} project folders; using all of them",
              file=sys.stderr)

    agent_files = []
    for d in session_dirs:
        agent_files.extend(sorted(d.rglob("*.jsonl")))

    return (mains, agent_files)


def source_label(path: Path, session_id: str) -> str:
    if path.name == f"{session_id}.jsonl":
        return "main"
    stem = path.stem  # e.g. agent-a1b2c3
    meta = path.with_suffix(".meta.json")
